REVIEW published: 28 April 2015 doi: 10.3389/fpls.2015.00259 [SOURCE: Page_2]

Effects of environmental factors and management practices on microclimate, winter physiology, and frost resistance in trees Guillaume Charrier 1* , Jérôme Ngao 2,3 , Marc Saudreau 2,3 and Thierry Améglio 2,3 1 Department of Botany, University of Innsbruck, Innsbruck, Austria, 2 INRA, Clermont-Ferrand, France, 3 Clermont Université, Université Blaise Pascal, Clermont-Ferrand, France [SOURCE: Page_2]

Freezing stress is one of the most important limiting factors determining the ecological distribution and production of tree species. Assessment of frost risk is, therefore, critical for forestry, fruit production, and horticulture. Frost risk is substantial when hazard (i.e., exposure to damaging freezing temperatures) intersects with vulnerability (i.e., frost sensitivity). Based on a large number of studies on frost resistance and frost occurrence, we highlight the complex interactive roles of environmental conditions, carbohydrates, Edited by: and water status in frost risk development. To supersede the classical empirical relations Glenn T. Howe, Oregon State University, USA        used to model frost hardiness, we propose an integrated ecophysiologically-based Reviewed by:       framework of frost risk assessment. This framework details the individual or interactive Cristina Nabais,    roles of these factors, and how they are distributed in time and space at the individual- University of Coimbra, Portugal Cyrille B. K. Rathgeber, tree level (within-crown and across organs). Based on this general framework, we are able Institut National de la Recherche      to highlight factors by which different environmental conditions (e.g., temperature, light, Agronomique, France flood, and drought), and management practices (pruning, thinning, girdling, sheltering, *Correspondence: water aspersion, irrigation, and fertilization) influence frost sensitivity and frost exposure Guillaume Charrier, Department of Botany, University of       of trees. Innsbruck, Sternwartestrasse 15, Innsbruck A-6020, Austria        Keywords: frost resistance, carbon balance, water status, stress interaction, microclimate, environmental factors, [EMAIL_REDACTED]       anthropogenic impact, risk assessment [SOURCE: Page_2]

Specialty section:       Introduction This article was submitted to Functional Plant Ecology,      Frost-related stresses strongly influence altitudinal and latitudinal plant distributions (Gusta et al., a section of the journal     1983; Koerner, 1998; Gansert, 2004; Larcher, 2005; Charrier et al., 2013a). Of different weather Frontiers in Plant Science      hazards, freezing events cause the greatest economic losses in agriculture (Snyder and Melo-Abreu, Received: 30 August 2014          2005; Snyder et al., 2005). A single freezing event can cause losses of hundreds of millions of dollars Accepted: 01 April 2015          in fruit and tree production (Attaway, 1997). Most fruit species currently growing in temperate zones Published: 28 April 2015         originate from warmer climates, especially Asia, e.g., walnut (Fornari et al., 2001), apple, pear, and Citation:      plum trees. In the selective breeding process that began in the Pliocene (Manchester, 1989), high Charrier G, Ngao J, Saudreau M and          yield and strong pathogen resistance were the principal goals, rather than frost resistance (Fady et al., Améglio T (2015) Effects of       2003). Although frost dramatically constrains life forms and generates enormous economic losses, it environmental factors and has not been as thoroughly studied as other biotic or abiotic stresses such as drought or herbivory. management practices on microclimate, winter physiology, and The main reason for this may be that damage occurs when trees seem to be inactive, and damage frost resistance in trees.    may become visible only in the subsequent growing season. Frost risk (probability of frost damage) increases when hazard (probability of a given freezing doi: 10.3389/fpls.2015.00259         temperature) coincides with vulnerability (frost sensitivity). Frost sensitivity is the converse of frost [SOURCE: Page_2]
